    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 59.11%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 2.4 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 15.14 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.59 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 59.11%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 32.0 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency   
          on a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited  
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 86.5% of the total average of 37.0 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 51.35%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 2.1 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 15.08 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.86 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 51.35%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 25.5 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency   
          on a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited  
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 82.1% of the total average of 31.0 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.

          on each stall reason.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 48.64%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 2.1 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 15.16 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.97 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 48.64%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 24.8 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency   
          on a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited  
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 79.7% of the total average of 31.1 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 15.94%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 2.4 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 14.80 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.65 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 15.94%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 30.2 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency   
          on a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited  
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 83.2% of the total average of 36.3 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.

          sampling data. The Profiling Guide                                                                            
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-reference) provides more details    
          on each stall reason.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 37.03%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 2.2 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 14.51 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.97 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 37.03%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 25.1 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency   
          on a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited  
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 79.4% of the total average of 31.6 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 11.54%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 6.2 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 13.72 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.36 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 11.54%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 61.5 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency   
          on a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited  
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 72.8% of the total average of 84.5 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 25.4%                                                                                     
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 2.5 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 14.45 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.84 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 25.4%                                                                                           
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 29.2 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency   
          on a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited  
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 81.6% of the total average of 35.8 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.

          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-reference) provides more details    
          on each stall reason.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 58.28%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 2.5 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 15.35 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.63 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 58.28%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 32.8 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency   
          on a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited  
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 84.5% of the total average of 38.8 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 37.2%                                                                                     
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 2.6 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 15.00 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.60 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 37.2%                                                                                           
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 34.6 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency   
          on a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited  
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 87.6% of the total average of 39.4 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 9.341%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 4.8 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 15.42 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.30 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 9.341%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 67.6 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency   
          on a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited  
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 91.7% of the total average of 73.7 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.
